CTA: Request a quote.
02. Prevention
    Sell prevention, not just repair. Explain that timely moving wild raccoons or squirrels safely can help prevent bites, structural damage, contaminated insulation, young animals left behind, and repeat entry. Hook: “A small warning sign today can become a much larger job later.” CTA: Get the issue checked and priced now.
03. Cost of Delay
    Frame inaction as a measurable business decision. Show the homeowner what waiting can expose them to: bites, structural damage, contaminated insulation, young animals left behind, and repeat entry. Hook: “The cheapest time to deal with this is usually before the damage spreads.” CTA: Compare local quotes before delaying.
04. Fast Relief / Speed
    Emphasize response time and momentum. Use scratching overhead, visible entry hole, roof damage, or animal sighting as the moment to act. Hook: “Need a solution without spending the next week calling contractors?” Promise fast matching, fast quoting, and a clear next step without promising unrealistic completion times.
05. Trust & Professionalism
    Reduce contractor anxiety. Center the message on inspection findings, pest identification, treatment/exclusion plan, follow-up terms, and safe-use instructions. Hook: “Know who is coming, what is being quoted, and what the job includes.” CTA: Compare professionals on scope, not just price.
06. Local Expertise
    Localize the message to weather, housing stock, bylaws, typical property types, and service availability in the neighbourhood. Hook: “Find moving wild raccoons or squirrels safely providers who actually serve your area.” CTA: Enter postal code and project details.
07. Safety
    Make the safety case concrete: poorly handled work can contribute to bites, structural damage, contaminated insulation, young animals left behind, and repeat entry. Hook: “Some home jobs are expensive when done twice—and dangerous when done wrong.” CTA: Get a qualified professional quote.
08. Property Value
    Connect the project to protecting or improving the property. Explain how humane, lawful removal with attention to how the animal entered can support maintenance, buyer confidence, usability, or curb appeal. Hook: “Protect the house you already paid for.” CTA: Price the upgrade.
09. Comfort / Quality of Life
    Sell the day-to-day benefit, not the technical work. Translate wildlife removal into a better living experience: humane, lawful removal with attention to how the animal entered. Hook: “The real product is a home that works better every day.” CTA: Tell us what you want improved.
10. Convenience / Done-for-You
    Target customers who value time. Hook: “Describe the job once instead of calling company after company.” Position Quotes.pro as the intake layer that turns one project description into comparable contractor conversations.
11. Family / Household Protection
    Frame the service around protecting the people using the home. Tie the message to avoiding bites, structural damage, contaminated insulation, young animals left behind, and repeat entry and achieving humane, lawful removal with attention to how the animal entered. Hook: “Fix the house problem before it becomes a household problem.” CTA: Request local help.
12. Efficiency / Operating Cost
    Highlight operating efficiency where relevant: wasted water, electricity, heating, cooling, maintenance time, or repeated service calls. Hook: “A proper fix can cost less than living with an inefficient problem month after month.” CTA: Compare repair and upgrade options.
13. Seasonal / Timing
    Create seasonal campaigns around the most natural trigger: scratching overhead, visible entry hole, roof damage, or animal sighting. Hook: “Do it before the busy season, not during it.” Adapt the copy by climate and month for each Canadian region.
14. Guarantee / Risk Reversal
    Use risk reversal carefully: promote written scopes, workmanship terms, warranties where offered, and documented expectations. Hook: “No vague handshake job—get the scope in writing.” Never claim a guarantee unless the contractor actually provides it.
15. Before-and-After Transformation
    Use visual transformation. Show the 'before' state implied by animals in attics, chimneys, soffits, sheds, or wall cavities, then the 'after' state: humane, lawful removal with attention to how the animal entered. Hook: “From problem area to finished result.” Best channels: short video, carousel, photo ad, landing-page slider.
16. Transparent Quote
    Make pricing transparency the offer. Hook: “Know what is included before you say yes.” Encourage itemized or clearly scoped quotes, site-visit requirements where needed, exclusions, material assumptions, and estimated timelines.
17. DIY vs Professional
    Contrast DIY effort with professional execution without insulting the homeowner. Hook: “You can spend the weekend troubleshooting—or get a professional to diagnose and quote it properly.” Mention specialized tools, code, safety, access, or finish quality when relevant.
18. Landlord / Property Manager
    Create a B2B version for landlords, property managers, real-estate teams, and small portfolios. Hook: “One property issue is a repair; ten properties need a system.” Emphasize repeat service, documentation, scheduling, and quote consistency.
19. New Homeowner / Move-In
    Target new owners and movers. Hook: “Just got the keys? Fix the unknowns before they become your first surprise bill.” Tie moving wild raccoons or squirrels safely to inspection follow-ups, move-in upgrades, and making the property fit the owner.
20. Preparedness / Future-Proofing
    Position the service as future-proofing. Hook: “Fix today’s issue in a way that makes the next upgrade easier.” Connect the project to future renovations, capacity, maintenance access, resale, resilience, or changing household needs.
21. Budget / Financing / Phasing
    Address price sensitivity directly. Hook: “Get the right scope first, then decide what to do now and what can wait.” Encourage alternate materials, phased work, repair-vs-replace comparisons, and financing only where a real provider offers it.
22. Social Proof / Reputation
    Use reputation as proof: verified reviews, project photos, local references, years in trade, repeat customers, and response history. Hook: “See evidence before you invite someone into your home.” Never fabricate ratings, testimonials, or project counts.

Hook: “From problem area to finished result.” Best channels: short video, carousel, photo ad, landing-page slider.
16. Transparent Quote
    Make pricing transparency the offer. Hook: “Know what is included before you say yes.” Encourage itemized or clearly scoped quotes, site-visit requirements where needed, exclusions, material assumptions, and estimated timelines.
17. DIY vs Professional
    Contrast DIY effort with professional execution without insulting the homeowner. Hook: “You can spend the weekend troubleshooting—or get a professional to diagnose and quote it properly.” Mention specialized tools, code, safety, access, or finish quality when relevant.
18. Landlord / Property Manager
    Create a B2B version for landlords, property managers, real-estate teams, and small portfolios. Hook: “One property issue is a repair; ten properties need a system.” Emphasize repeat service, documentation, scheduling, and quote consistency.
19. New Homeowner / Move-In
    Target new owners and movers. Hook: “Just got the keys? Fix the unknowns before they become your first surprise bill.” Tie planning and testing (smart help) to inspection follow-ups, move-in upgrades, and making the property fit the owner.
20. Preparedness / Future-Proofing
    Position the service as future-proofing. Hook: “Fix today’s issue in a way that makes the next upgrade easier.” Connect the project to future renovations, capacity, maintenance access, resale, resilience, or changing household needs.
21. Budget / Financing / Phasing
    Address price sensitivity directly. Hook: “Get the right scope first, then decide what to do now and what can wait.” Encourage alternate materials, phased work, repair-vs-replace comparisons, and financing only where a real provider offers it.
22. Social Proof / Reputation
    Use reputation as proof: verified reviews, project photos, local references, years in trade, repeat customers, and response history. Hook: “See evidence before you invite someone into your home.” Never fabricate ratings, testimonials, or project counts.

Hook: “Need a solution without spending the next week calling contractors?” Promise fast matching, fast quoting, and a clear next step without promising unrealistic completion times.

    Reduce contractor anxiety. Center the message on credentials, reports, drawings, testing methodology, documented assumptions, references, and clear deliverables. Hook: “Know who is coming, what is being quoted, and what the job includes.” CTA: Compare professionals on scope, not just price.

Hook: “Need a solution without spending the next week calling contractors?” Promise fast matching, fast quoting, and a clear next step without promising unrealistic completion times.
05. Trust & Professionalism
    Reduce contractor anxiety. Center the message on credentials, reports, drawings, testing methodology, documented assumptions, references, and clear deliverables. Hook: “Know who is coming, what is being quoted, and what the job includes.” CTA: Compare professionals on scope, not just price.
06. Local Expertise
    Localize the message to weather, housing stock, bylaws, typical property types, and service availability in the neighbourhood. Hook: “Find planning beautiful yards (garden designers) providers who actually serve your area.” CTA: Enter postal code and project details.
07. Safety
    Make the safety case concrete: poorly handled work can contribute to dead plants, poor drainage, overcrowding, expensive rework, and disconnected hardscape. Hook: “Some home jobs are expensive when done twice—and dangerous when done wrong.” CTA: Get a qualified professional quote.
08. Property Value
    Connect the project to protecting or improving the property. Explain how a coherent outdoor plan that balances beauty, maintenance, climate, and use can support maintenance, buyer confidence, usability, or curb appeal. Hook: “Protect the house you already paid for.” CTA: Price the upgrade.
09. Comfort / Quality of Life
    Sell the day-to-day benefit, not the technical work. Translate landscape and garden design into a better living experience: a coherent outdoor plan that balances beauty, maintenance, climate, and use. Hook: “The real product is a home that works better every day.” CTA: Tell us what you want improved.
10. Convenience / Done-for-You
    Target customers who value time. Hook: “Describe the job once instead of calling company after company.” Position Quotes.pro as the intake layer that turns one project description into comparable contractor conversations.
11. Family / Household Protection
    Frame the service around protecting the people using the home. Tie the message to avoiding dead plants, poor drainage, overcrowding, expensive rework, and disconnected hardscape and achieving a coherent outdoor plan that balances beauty, maintenance, climate, and use. Hook: “Fix the house problem before it becomes a household problem.” CTA: Request local help.
12. Efficiency / Operating Cost
    Highlight operating efficiency where relevant: wasted water, electricity, heating, cooling, maintenance time, or repeated service calls. Hook: “A proper fix can cost less than living with an inefficient problem month after month.” CTA: Compare repair and upgrade options.
13. Seasonal / Timing
    Create seasonal campaigns around the most natural trigger: new home, major yard renovation, pool/deck project, curb-appeal plan, or planting overhaul. Hook: “Do it before the busy season, not during it.” Adapt the copy by climate and month for each Canadian region.
14. Guarantee / Risk Reversal
    Use risk reversal carefully: promote written scopes, workmanship terms, warranties where offered, and documented expectations. Hook: “No vague handshake job—get the scope in writing.” Never claim a guarantee unless the contractor actually provides it.
15. Before-and-After Transformation
    Use visual transformation. Show the 'before' state implied by random planting, poor yard flow, wasted space, drainage concerns, and uncertainty about what belongs where, then the 'after' state: a coherent outdoor plan that balances beauty, maintenance, climate, and use. Hook: “From problem area to finished result.” Best channels: short video, carousel, photo ad, landing-page slider.
16. Transparent Quote
    Make pricing transparency the offer. Hook: “Know what is included before you say yes.” Encourage itemized or clearly scoped quotes, site-visit requirements where needed, exclusions, material assumptions, and estimated timelines.
17. DIY vs Professional
    Contrast DIY effort with professional execution without insulting the homeowner. Hook: “You can spend the weekend troubleshooting—or get a professional to diagnose and quote it properly.” Mention specialized tools, code, safety, access, or finish quality when relevant.
18. Landlord / Property Manager
    Create a B2B version for landlords, property managers, real-estate teams, and small portfolios. Hook: “One property issue is a repair; ten properties need a system.” Emphasize repeat service, documentation, scheduling, and quote consistency.
19. New Homeowner / Move-In
    Target new owners and movers. Hook: “Just got the keys? Fix the unknowns before they become your first surprise bill.” Tie planning beautiful yards (garden designers) to inspection follow-ups, move-in upgrades, and making the property fit the owner.
20. Preparedness / Future-Proofing
    Position the service as future-proofing. Hook: “Fix today’s issue in a way that makes the next upgrade easier.” Connect the project to future renovations, capacity, maintenance access, resale, resilience, or changing household needs.
21. Budget / Financing / Phasing
    Address price sensitivity directly. Hook: “Get the right scope first, then decide what to do now and what can wait.” Encourage alternate materials, phased work, repair-vs-replace comparisons, and financing only where a real provider offers it.
22. Social Proof / Reputation
    Use reputation as proof: verified reviews, project photos, local references, years in trade, repeat customers, and response history. Hook: “See evidence before you invite someone into your home.” Never fabricate ratings, testimonials, or project counts.


====================================================================================================
CATEGORY 9.6 | Picking paint colors and furniture layouts
====================================================================================================
NODE TYPE: SERVICE SUB-BRANCH
WHAT IT MEANS: interior design and space planning.
COMMON CUSTOMER PAIN: decision overload, awkward furniture placement, mismatched finishes, and uncertainty about colour or scale.
DESIRED OUTCOME: a more cohesive interior plan with clearer selections before money is spent.
MAIN RISK / OBJECTION: buying the wrong furniture, repainting, poor flow, and expensive design changes after installation.
BEST BUYING TRIGGERS: move-in, renovation, room refresh, new furniture purchase, or whole-home style update.
TRUST SIGNALS TO SHOW: credentials, reports, drawings, testing methodology, documented assumptions, references, and clear deliverables.

22 MARKETING ANGLES
01. Pain / Problem
    Lead with the problem the customer already feels: decision overload, awkward furniture placement, mismatched finishes, and uncertainty about colour or scale. Hook: “Dealing with decision overload, awkward furniture placement, mismatched finishes, and uncertainty about colour or scale? Get a local quote for picking paint colors and furniture layouts before the problem becomes harder to ignore.” Position the service as the direct path to a more cohesive interior plan with clearer selections before money is spent. CTA: Request a quote.
02. Prevention
    Sell prevention, not just repair. Explain that timely picking paint colors and furniture layouts can help prevent buying the wrong furniture, repainting, poor flow, and expensive design changes after installation. Hook: “A small warning sign today can become a much larger job later.” CTA: Get the issue checked and priced now.
03. Cost of Delay
    Frame inaction as a measurable business decision. Show the homeowner what waiting can expose them to: buying the wrong furniture, repainting, poor flow, and expensive design changes after installation. Hook: “The cheapest time to deal with this is usually before the damage spreads.” CTA: Compare local quotes before delaying.
04. Fast Relief / Speed
    Emphasize response time and momentum. Use move-in, renovation, room refresh, new furniture purchase, or whole-home style update as the moment to act. Hook: “Need a solution without spending the next week calling contractors?” Promise fast matching, fast quoting, and a clear next step without promising unrealistic completion times.

23. Longueuil

24. Brossard

26. Saint-Hubert

27. Repentigny

LOCATION MICRO-MAPPING RULE
---------------------------
Build 27 meaningful local zones under every regional hub using population, housing density,
travel patterns, homeowner concentration, service coverage, and recognizable local names.
Do not create fake neighbourhood names merely to reach 27. Where a region has fewer strong
neighbourhood identities, use municipalities, boroughs, adjacent towns, or service zones.

Every local landing page should contain genuinely local information such as:
- housing types common in the area;
- climate or seasonal service triggers;
- common age range of housing stock where known;
- service radius and contractor availability;
- local permit/licensing notes when relevant;
- region-specific terminology;
- actual nearby municipalities or boroughs;
- local project examples when the platform has them.

Avoid mass-producing hundreds of near-identical pages with only the city name swapped.

====================================================================================================
PART 4 — SCALE OF THE MARKETING SYSTEM
====================================================================================================

CORE SERVICE NODES
------------------
3 specialty roots
+ 9 main category branches
+ 54 service sub-branches
= 66 total marketing nodes

LOCAL MARKETS
-------------
18 regional hubs x 27 local zones each = 486 local markets

POTENTIAL SERVICE x LOCATION COMBINATIONS
-----------------------------------------
66 service nodes x 486 local markets = 32,076 service-location combinations

POTENTIAL SERVICE x LOCATION x MARKETING-ANGLE COMBINATIONS
------------------------------------------------------------
32,076 service-location combinations x 22 marketing angles
= 705,672 possible campaign combinations

That number is a campaign inventory, not a recommendation to publish 705,672 indexable SEO pages.
A strong operating model would select the highest-value combinations based on search demand,
contractor coverage, lead value, seasonality, competition, and conversion rate.

====================================================================================================
PART 5 — RECOMMENDED PAGE / CAMPAIGN FORMULA
====================================================================================================

PAGE TITLE FORMULA
------------------
[Service] in [Neighbourhood / City] | Compare Local Quotes | Quotes.pro

H1 FORMULA
----------
Get Quotes for [Service] in [Location]

ABOVE-THE-FOLD COPY
-------------------
1. State the problem in normal homeowner language.
2. State the outcome.
3. Ask 3-7 qualifying questions.
4. Collect postal code early.
5. Explain what happens after submission.
6. Show proof and contractor-selection criteria.
7. Use one primary CTA: Request Quotes.

QUOTE-FORM FIELDS
-----------------
- Postal code
- Property type
- Owner / tenant / property manager
- Service category
- Specific service
- Repair / replacement / new installation / emergency
- Short description
- Photos or video
- Desired timing
- Budget range (optional)
- Preferred contact method
- Best contact time
- Permission to share the request with matching professionals

LEAD-QUALITY QUESTIONS
----------------------
- Is the issue active right now?
- Is there visible damage?
- Has another contractor already inspected it?
- Is the customer looking for repair or replacement?
- Does the project require materials to be supplied?
- Is access available?
- Is this an insurance-related project?
- Does the customer own the property?
- Is there a target completion date?
- Is the customer ready to hire if the quote fits?

CONTRACTOR-SIDE POSITIONING
---------------------------
Market Quotes.pro to professionals around lead quality rather than raw lead volume:
- local requests;
- clear service classification;
- photos before the first call;
- customer timing;
- property type;
- urgency;
- job description;
- reduced time wasted on poor-fit inquiries;
- opportunity to quote recurring property managers separately.

====================================================================================================
PART 6 — CONTENT ENGINE
====================================================================================================

FOR EACH SERVICE NODE, THE 22 ANGLES CAN BE TURNED INTO A CONTENT SET:
- 22 short-form video concepts
- 22 X post concepts
- 22 paid-ad concepts
- 22 email/newsletter topics
- 22 blog or FAQ concepts
- 22 landing-page sections or A/B tests

One service sub-branch can therefore support 132 distinct content assets before localization.
Across 54 service sub-branches, that is 7,128 base content assets before adding local variants.

A practical publishing rule:
- Use root pages for broad discovery.
- Use category pages for navigation and authority.
- Use service sub-branch pages for high-intent conversion.
- Use local pages only where actual contractor coverage exists.
- Use the 22 angles as creative variants, not as duplicate doorway pages.

====================================================================================================
PART 7 — BRAND MESSAGE
====================================================================================================

HOUSEPRO.SERVICES
Consumer-facing umbrella: understand the job, find the right service, and move from problem to solution.

QUOTES.PRO
Transaction-focused promise: describe the job once, compare relevant local quotes, and choose the provider yourself.

SIMPLE CORE COPY
----------------
“Tell us what your home needs. We organize the request, match it to the right service category,
and help you connect with local professionals who can quote the work.”

TRUST PRINCIPLE
---------------
The platform should never imply that the lowest quote is automatically the best quote.
The user should compare:
- scope;
- materials;
- exclusions;
- availability;
- licensing where required;
- insurance;
- warranty;
- reviews;
- experience;
- communication;
- total price.

END OF MASTER ARCHITECTURE
